**Jair Messias Bolsonaro** (Brazilian Portuguese: [\[ʒaˈiʁ meˈsi.ɐz bowsoˈnaɾu\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help\:IPA/Portuguese) [//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/transcoded/9/92/LL-Q5146\_%28por%29-Juwan\_%28JnpoJuwan%29-Jair_Messias_Bolsonaro.wav/LL-Q5146\_%28por%29-Juwan\_%28JnpoJuwan%29-Jair_Messias_Bolsonaro.wav.mp3](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/transcoded/9/92/LL-Q5146_%28por%29-Juwan_%28JnpoJuwan%29-Jair_Messias_Bolsonaro.wav/LL-Q5146_%28por%29-Juwan_%28JnpoJuwan%29-Jair_Messias_Bolsonaro.wav.mp3)<sup>ⓘ</sup>; born 21 March 1955) is a Brazilian politician and former [military officer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Military_officer) who served as the 38th [president of Brazil](273638.md) from 2019 to 2023. He previously served as a member of the [Chamber of Deputies](1589485.md) from 1991 to 2019.

Born in [Glicério](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Glic%C3%A9rio), São Paulo, Bolsonaro began serving in the [Brazilian Army](1047559.md) in 1973 and graduated from the [Military Academy of Agulhas Negras](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Military_Academy_of_Agulhas_Negras) in 1977.[^cite_note-1] He attracted publicity in 1986, after he wrote an article for *[Veja](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Veja_\(magazine\))* magazine criticizing the low wages of military officers, after which he was arrested and detained for fifteen days. He left the army, and was elected to the [Municipal Chamber of Rio de Janeiro](22849342.md) two years later. In 1990, he was first elected to the Chamber of Deputies as a representative for the [state of Rio de Janeiro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/State_of_Rio_de_Janeiro). During his 27-year tenure as a congressman, he became known for his [national conservatism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_conservatism). Bolsonaro entered the [2018 presidential election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018_Brazilian_presidential_election), during which he began claiming to support [economically liberal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Economically_liberal) and pro-market policies.[^cite_note-2] After surviving [an assassination attempt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Attempted_assassination_of_Jair_Bolsonaro) on 6 September, he led in the 7 October first-round results and defeated [Fernando Haddad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fernando_Haddad) in the 28 October runoff.

Bolsonaro focused on domestic affairs in his first months as president, dealing primarily with the fallout of the [2014 Brazilian economic crisis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2014_Brazilian_economic_crisis). The [economy](3634.md) recovered slowly, while crime rates fell sharply during the first year.[^cite_note-3][^cite_note-4] He rolled back protections for [Indigenous groups](55510.md) in the [Amazon rainforest](48139.md)[^cite_note-Indigenous_Global_Affairs-5] and facilitated its [deforestation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deforestation_in_Brazil).[^cite_note-environment-6] Bolsonaro's response to the [COVID-19 pandemic in Brazil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/COVID-19_pandemic_in_Brazil) received significant criticism after he sought to downplay the pandemic and its effects, opposed quarantine measures, and dismissed two health ministers, while the death toll increased rapidly.[^cite_note-7]

A polarizing politician, Bolsonaro has drawn both praise and criticism in Brazil for his views and comments, which have been described as [far-right](221220.md) and [populist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Populist).[^cite_note-nyt-8][^cite_note-9][^cite_note-10][^cite_note-veconomist-11] He is a vocal opponent of [same-sex marriage](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Same-sex_marriage_in_Brazil),[^cite_note-noticias.terra.com.br-12][^cite_note-13] [abortion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abortion_in_Brazil),[^cite_note-osul.com.br-14] [affirmative action](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Affirmative_action),[^cite_note-Política-15] [drug decriminalization](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Drug_liberalization), and state [secularism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Secularism).[^cite_note-epoca2-16] In foreign policy, he has advocated closer relations [with Israel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brazil%E2%80%93Israel_relations) and [with the United States](11633777.md);[^cite_note-\:4-17][^cite_note-\:3-18] later in his presidency, he also made efforts to improve relations with the [BRICS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BRICS) countries.[^cite_note-19][^cite_note-russia-20]

In the [2022 general election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_Brazilian_general_election) Bolsonaro lost to [Luiz Inácio Lula da Silva](99328.md).[^cite_note-21] On 8 January 2023, a mob of Bolsonaro's supporters [stormed federal government buildings](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/8_January_Bras%C3%ADlia_attacks), calling for a [coup d'état](25914154.md). On 30 June, the [Superior Electoral Court](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Superior_Electoral_Court) blocked Bolsonaro from seeking office until 2030 for attempting to undermine the validity of the election through his unfounded claims of voter fraud, and for abusing his power by using government communication channels to both promote his campaign and to allege fraud. Testimony from military officials showed that Bolsonaro had [planned a self-coup](76043560.md) with the military to keep himself in power.[^cite_note-22]

In November 2024, he [was indicted](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operation_Counter-coup) by the [Federal Police of Brazil](2361410.md) of multiple counts related to the planned coup.[^cite_note-23] He was charged in February 2025,[^cite_note-24] and the Supreme Court ruled he must stand trial.[^cite_note-25] On 4 August 2025, he was placed under [house arrest](247643.md) for using a mobile phone and accessing social media via his son [Eduardo](58054973.md),[^cite_note-26] violating pre-trial rules.[^cite_note-27] His trial began on 2 September; on 11 September he was found guilty by the [Supreme Federal Court](3359076.md) and [sentenced to 27 years and 3 months](81038610.md) in prison.[^cite_note-28][^cite_note-29] On 22 November 2025, [Bolsonaro was arrested](81665132.md) by the Federal Police after trying to remove his electronic ankle monitor.[^cite_note-federalcustody-30][^cite_note-nov22arrest-31][^cite_note-prisonorder-32] On 25 November 2025, the Supreme Court rejected his final appeals, declared the case to have reached [res judicata](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Res_judicata) and ordered the beginning of the enforcement of the prison sentence.[^cite_note-\:12-33] On 24 March 2026, he was granted 90 days of temporary house arrest due to health issues, which was later extended.[^cite_note-\:13-34][^cite_note-cnnbrasil.com.br-35]

## Early life

Jair Messias Bolsonaro was born on 21 March 1955 in [Glicério](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Glic%C3%A9rio), São Paulo,[^cite_note-36] in [southeast](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southeast_Region,_Brazil) Brazil, to Percy Geraldo Bolsonaro and Olinda Bonturi.[^cite_note-37][^cite_note-38] His family is mostly of [Italian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Italian_Brazilians) descent, with [German](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/German_Brazilians) ancestry as well. On his father's side, he is the great-grandson of Italians from [Veneto](43780.md) and [Calabria](44772.md).[^cite_note-genealogia-39] Bolsonaro's paternal grandfather's family comes from Veneto, more precisely [Anguillara Veneta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anguillara_Veneta), in the [province of Padua](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Province_of_Padua). His great-grandfather Vittorio Bolzonaro (the surname was originally written with a *"z"*), was born on 12 April 1878. Vittorio's parents immigrated to Brazil when he was ten, together with his siblings, Giovanna and Tranquillo.[^cite_note-40][^cite_note-genealogia-39] His German ancestry came from his father's maternal grandfather, Carl "Carlos" Hintze, born in [Hamburg](13467.md) around 1876, who immigrated to Brazil in 1883.[^cite_note-genealogia-39] His maternal grandparents were born in [Lucca](46169.md), in [Tuscany](21967242.md), and went to live in Brazil in the 1890s.[^cite_note-genealogia-39] On 21 January 2022 his mother, Olinda Bonturi Bolsonaro, died at age 94. His father, Percy Geraldo Bolsonaro, died in 1995.[^cite_note-41]

Bolsonaro spent most of his childhood moving around São Paulo with his family, living in [Ribeira](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ribeira,_S%C3%A3o_Paulo), [Jundiaí](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jundia%C3%AD), and [Sete Barras](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sete_Barras), before settling in [Eldorado](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eldorado,_S%C3%A3o_Paulo), in the state's southern region, in 1966, where he grew up with his five siblings.[^cite_note-42] His first name is a tribute to [Jair da Rosa Pinto](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jair_da_Rosa_Pinto), a football player for [Palmeiras](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sociedade_Esportiva_Palmeiras), with whom he shares a birthday.[^cite_note-43][^cite_note-44]

## Military career



Jair (left) with his parents at the Military Academy of Agulhas Negras in 1979



Bolsonaro in 1986

In his final years in high school Bolsonaro was admitted to the [Escola Preparatória de Cadetes do Exército](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Escola_Preparat%C3%B3ria_de_Cadetes_do_Ex%C3%A9rcito) (the prep school of the [Brazilian Army](1047559.md)), which he entered in 1973. In 1974 he went to the [Military Academy of Agulhas Negras](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Military_Academy_of_Agulhas_Negras) (Brazil's premier military academy), graduating in 1977 as an [artillery](2508.md) officer. He served in the 9th Field Artillery Group, in [Nioaque](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nioaque), [Mato Grosso do Sul](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mato_Grosso_do_Sul). Later, he studied at the Army Physical Training School in [Rio de Janeiro](25936.md) and served in the 21st Field Artillery Group and the 8th Paratrooper Field Artillery Group, from the [Paratrooper Brigade](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paratroopers_Brigade_\(Brazil\)), also based in Rio. His superior officers described him as "aggressive" and criticized his "excessive ambition for financial and economic gain". This assessment referred to Bolsonaro's attempt to mine gold in [Bahia](199070.md), though he later claimed the activity was merely a "hobby and mental hygiene".[^cite_note-nyt-8][^cite_note-45]

In 1987 he studied in the [Officers Improvement School](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Officers_Improvement_School?action=edit\&redlink=1), where he completed the Artillery Advanced Course. Bolsonaro first caught the public's attention in 1986 when he wrote [an article](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/O_sal%C3%A1rio_est%C3%A1_baixo) published in the news magazine *[Veja](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Veja_\(magazine\))*. He complained about low military salaries and claimed that the High Command was dismissing officers due to budgetary cuts, not because they were displaying "deviations of conduct", as the command had told the press.[^cite_note-46] Despite being reprimanded by his superiors, Bolsonaro received praise from fellow officers and wives of [military men](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brazilian_Armed_Forces), becoming a household name for supporters who were growing disenchanted with Brazil's new civilian democratic government.[^cite_note-47]

In October 1987 Bolsonaro faced a new accusation. *Veja* reported that, with an Army colleague, he had plans to plant bombs in military units in [Rio de Janeiro](25936.md). After Bolsonaro called the allegation "a fantasy", the magazine published, in its next issue, sketches allegedly made by Bolsonaro in which the plan was detailed. Official records unearthed by the newspaper *[O Estado de S. Paulo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/O_Estado_de_S._Paulo)* in 2018 detailed the case. After an investigation by an administrative military bureau called a Justification Board, Bolsonaro was unanimously found guilty. According to the board, Bolsonaro had a "serious personality deviation and a professional deformation", "lack of moral courage to leave the Army" and "lied throughout the process" when denying frequent contacts with *Veja*. The Supreme Military Court then analysed the case. The general in charge of reporting the case voted to acquit Bolsonaro, arguing that he had already been penalized for the initial *Veja* article, that there was no testimonial evidence of his plans to plant bombs, and that there were "deep contradictions in the four graphological exams", two of which failed to conclude that Bolsonaro was the author of the sketches. Bolsonaro was acquitted by the majority of the court (9 v 4 votes). In December 1988, just after this ruling, he left the Army to begin his political career. He served in the military for 15 years, reaching the rank of captain.[^cite_note-48]

## Early political career

### City councilor in Rio de Janeiro (1989–1991)



Bolsonaro as [Rio de Janeiro](25936.md) city councilor in March 1990. In October of the same year, he was elected as a federal deputy for the city.

Bolsonaro entered politics in 1988, when he was elected to serve as a [city councilor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/City_councilor) in Rio de Janeiro, representing the [Christian Democratic Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christian_Democratic_Party_\(Brazil\)) (PDC). According to the biography by his son Flávio, Bolsonaro "was a candidate for councilor because it happened to be the only option he had at the moment to avoid persecution by some superiors. His entry into politics happened by chance, for his desire was to continue in his military career".[^cite_note-book-49]

Bolsonaro spent only two years in the [Municipal Chamber of Rio de Janeiro](22849342.md). He was described as a quiet, discreet and conservative councilor, and showed little participation.[^cite_note-vereador-50] His term as councilor was used mainly to give visibility to military causes, such as retirement benefits for former officers.[^cite_note-book-49]

### Federal deputy for Rio de Janeiro (1991–2018)

In the [1990 Brazilian parliamentary election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1990_Brazilian_parliamentary_election) Bolsonaro was elected a [federal deputy](1589485.md) for the Christian Democratic Party. He served seven consecutive terms, from 1991 to 2018. He was affiliated with several other political parties over the years. In 2014, he was the congressman who gained the most votes in Rio de Janeiro, with 465,000.[^cite_note-51]

Bolsonaro's name was listed on the "[Lista de Furnas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lista_de_Furnas?action=edit\&redlink=1)", a list detailing a corruption and money laundering scheme involving the state-owned electricity company, [Eletrobras Furnas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eletrobras_Furnas).[^cite_note-52] He received housing assistance for deputies who do not have residences in the capital [Brasília](4752.md), despite having an apartment in the southwest of the city. H later admitted that he considers this practice of his "immoral".[^cite_note-53] He has also been accused of engaging in fuel allowance fraud.[^cite_note-54]

In his 27 years of service in the [National Congress](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Congress_of_Brazil), Bolsonaro put forward one constitutional amendment and at least 171 bills, two of which became law.[^cite_note-55] Bolsonaro, who claimed to be persecuted by the left-wing parties, said most congressmen do not vote according to their agenda, but "by who the author of the bill is".[^cite_note-56]

In January 2018 he abandoned the [Social Christian Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Social_Christian_Party_\(Brazil\)) and switched to the [Social Liberal Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Social_Liberal_Party_\(Brazil\)) (PSL). After his arrival, the PSL adopted conservative and right-wing positions, and its social liberal group *[Livres](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Livres_\(movement\))* announced its departure from the PSL.[^cite_note-57][^cite_note-58]

## Presidential campaign (2018)

Main article: [Jair Bolsonaro 2018 presidential campaign](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jair_Bolsonaro_2018_presidential_campaign)

On 22 July 2018, the PSL nominated Bolsonaro for president in the [2018 election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018_Brazilian_general_election).[^cite_note-59] The [Brazilian Labour Renewal Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brazilian_Labour_Renewal_Party) also endorsed him. His coalition name was "Brazil above everything, God above everyone" (*_Brasil acima de tudo, Deus acima de todos_*).[^cite_note-60][^cite_note-61] Though contested by two lawsuits, the [Superior Electoral Court](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Superior_Electoral_Court) of Brazil deferred them and his candidacy was made official on 6 August.[^cite_note-62] In August Bolsonaro announced that [Antônio Hamilton Mourão](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ant%C3%B4nio_Hamilton_Mour%C3%A3o), a retired army general, would be his running mate.[^cite_note-63]



Bolsonaro supporters in London, October 2018

According to political pundits, Bolsonaro moderated his tone early in the campaign, taking a less aggressive and confrontational style. Economically, he started to support less government intervention in the economy (in contrast to the past, when he defended [developmentalist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Developmentalism) policies). On the other hand, he maintained his tough stance on crime and his defence of "traditional family values".[^cite_note-64] Bolsonaro also said he planned to cut [taxes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taxation_in_Brazil) across the board, particularly on inheritances and businesses, to generate growth and tackle unemployment.[^cite_note-65] He also promised more [austerity](684037.md) measures and cuts in government spending, but had difficulty naming the areas where he would make cuts. He also said he would work to diminish the federal government's size and bureaucracy by enacting a wide variety of [deregulation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deregulation) measures.[^cite_note-66] Bolsonaro's promises to [restore security](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Law_and_order_\(politics\)) amid [record high crime](15348345.md) and to stamp out Brazil's rampant [political corruption](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Corruption_in_Brazil) won him huge popular support.[^cite_note-67] In October, he announced he would name [Paulo Guedes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paulo_Guedes), a [liberal economist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Liberal_economist), as his finance minister.[^cite_note-68]

On 9 August 2018 Bolsonaro attended the first presidential debate of the year, organized by the TV network [Rede Bandeirantes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rede_Bandeirantes).[^cite_note-69] A week later, there was another debate at [RedeTV!](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/RedeTV!)[^cite_note-70] On 28 August he gave an interview to *[Jornal Nacional](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jornal_Nacional)*, Brazil's highest-rated primetime news programme, at [Rede Globo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rede_Globo).[^cite_note-71] Bolsonaro was the first presidential candidate to raise over [R\$](34201323.md)1 million in donations from the public during the 2018 campaign. In the first 59 days, he amassed an average of R\$17,000 per day.[^cite_note-72]

After the [Workers' Party](224622.md) candidate [Luiz Inácio Lula da Silva](99328.md) was arrested in April 2018, Bolsonaro became the frontrunner according to all major opinion polls.[^cite_note-73][^cite_note-Disciple-74] A *[Datafolha](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Datafolha)* poll from September showed Bolsonaro as the leading candidate in the first round with 28% of vote intentions, though runoff scenarios showed him losing to [Geraldo Alckmin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Geraldo_Alckmin), [Fernando Haddad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fernando_Haddad), and [Ciro Gomes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ciro_Gomes) and tying with [Marina Silva](17484359.md).[^cite_note-75] Another *Datafolha* poll, conducted the week before election day, showed a considerable surge for Bolsonaro, who had 40% of vote intentions, or 36% when null or blank vote intentions were included. Haddad came in second with 25% and Gomes third with 15%.[^cite_note-76]



Bolsonaro is known for his [finger-gun](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Finger-gun) gesture, which he used during the presidential campaign.

In the first round of the election, on 7 October 2018, Bolsonaro finished in first place with 46% of the popular vote (49.2 million). Since he failed to win over 50%, he faced the second-place finisher, Haddad, in the second round, held on 28 October 2018.[^cite_note-77] After the first round, when his victory looked certain, Bolsonaro gave a speech by videolink to thousands of supporters who gathered at Paulista Avenue, in São Paulo. In the speech he threatened to arrest, purge, or kill "reds" and "petralhas" (a derogatory term for Workers' Party members), and promised that members of the social movements [MST](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Landless_Workers'_Movement) and [MTST](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Homeless_Workers'_Movement) would be treated as "terrorists". He said: "This time, the clean-up will be even greater. This group \["reds"], if they want to stay, will have to abide by our laws ... These red outlaws will be banned from our homeland. Either they go overseas, or they go to jail ... Petralhada, you all go to the edge of the beach. It will be a cleaning never seen in the history of Brazil".[^cite_note-78][^cite_note-79] The "edge of the beach", a Bolsonaro aide later confirmed, was a reference to a [Navy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brazilian_Navy) base at [Restinga da Marambaia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Restinga_da_Marambaia), in Rio de Janeiro State, where the [Brazilian military dictatorship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brazilian_military_dictatorship) tortured and killed dissidents.[^cite_note-uol.com.br-80] The speech was widely condemned by rivals, journalists and politicians.[^cite_note-81][^cite_note-82][^cite_note-83][^cite_note-84]

Bolsonaro won the second round of the election with 55.13% of the votes, and was elected the 38th president of Brazil.[^cite_note-85][^cite_note-86][^cite_note-87] He took office on 1 January 2019.[^cite_note-88] During the campaign, academics repeatedly raised concerns about the consequences to Brazilian democracy Bolsonaro's rise will have. In the news magazine *[Foreign Policy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Foreign_Policy)*, [Federico Finchelstein](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Federico_Finchelstein), a historian at the [New School for Social Research](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_School_for_Social_Research) who specializes in fascism, wrote, "Bolsonaro's vocabulary recalls the rhetoric behind Nazi policies of persecution and victimization. But does sounding like a Nazi make him a Nazi? Insomuch as he believes in holding elections, he is not there yet. However, things could change quickly if he gains power".[^cite_note-89] [Jason Stanley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jason_Stanley), a philosopher at [Yale University](34273.md) who has published widely on [Nazism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nazism), said that Bolsonaro "uses more tactics associated to fascism than \[the] American president [Donald Trump](4848272.md)".[^cite_note-90] [Steven Levitsky](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Steven_Levitsky), a political scientist at [Harvard University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Harvard_University), said that Bolsonaro "is clearly authoritarian", but not a fascist.[^cite_note-91] Similar concerns were raised by analysts in Portugal[^cite_note-92] and Brazil.[^cite_note-93] Others, such as the [Marxist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marxism) historian [Perry Anderson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Perry_Anderson), dismissed the "fascist" and "populist" labels altogether.[^cite_note-94]

Another highly controversial aspect of the campaign was the alleged use of illegal digital communication strategies by some of Bolsonaro's most important financial supporters. According to an investigation by *[Folha](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Folha_de_S.Paulo)*, one of Brazil's bestselling newspapers, "Bolsonaro has been getting an illegal helping hand from a group of Brazilian entrepreneurs who are bankrolling a campaign to bombard [WhatsApp](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/WhatsApp) users with [fake news](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fake_news) about Haddad".[^cite_note-95] The suspicions led to a formal investigation by electoral authorities and the Federal Police; Bolsonaro and allies denied any wrongdoing.[^cite_note-96] Another controversial point was that Taíse Feijó, an adviser in Bolsonaro's government, was among those paid to feed fake news to his supporters.[^cite_note-97]

### Attempted assassination

Main article: [Attempted assassination of Jair Bolsonaro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Attempted_assassination_of_Jair_Bolsonaro)



The moment Bolsonaro (center, yellow shirt) was attacked at the [Juiz de Fora](882285.md) rally

Bolsonaro was stabbed in the abdomen on 6 September 2018 while campaigning and interacting with supporters in [Juiz de Fora](882285.md), Minas Gerais.[^cite_note-Londono18-98] At first, his son [Flávio Bolsonaro](58640941.md) stated that his father's wounds were only superficial and he was recovering in the hospital,[^cite_note-99] but he later said the wounds seemed worse than initially thought[^cite_note-100] and his father most likely would not be able to start campaigning personally before the end of the first round.[^cite_note-101] He posted on [Twitter](9988187.md) about his father's condition, explaining that the perforation had reached parts of the liver, lung, and intestine. He also said that Bolsonaro had lost a large amount of blood, arriving at the hospital with severe [hypotension](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hypotension) (his [blood pressure](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Blood_pressure) was 10/3, equivalent to 100/30 mmHg), but that he had since stabilized.[^cite_note-102][^cite_note-103][^cite_note-Londono18-98] The attack was condemned by most of the other candidates in the presidential race, and by President [Michel Temer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michel_Temer).[^cite_note-104] The day after the attack, Bolsonaro was transferred to the [Albert Einstein Israelite Hospital](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Albert_Einstein_Israelite_Hospital) in São Paulo, after a request from his family. According to the doctors, he was in an "extremely stable" condition.[^cite_note-105]

Police arrested the attacker and identified him as Adélio Bispo de Oliveira, who, according to security agents, claimed he was on "a mission from God".[^cite_note-106] He had been a member of the [Socialism and Liberty Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Socialism_and_Liberty_Party) from 2007 to 2014. His social media posts included political criticisms of both Bolsonaro and Temer.[^cite_note-107] But an initial Federal Police investigation concluded that Adélio had no help from political organizations and acted alone.[^cite_note-108] A medical report produced for a second investigation concluded that Bispo is mentally disturbed, having a "permanent paranoid delusional disorder" which, according to Brazilian law, prevents him from being considered legally liable for his actions.[^cite_note-109] In a May 2019 decision the Federal Court found Bispo not liable. Bolsonaro did not appeal the decision.[^cite_note-\:7-110]

On 29 September, a month after the attack, Bolsonaro was released from the hospital and returned to his home in Rio de Janeiro. His condition prevented him from returning to the campaign trail for the remainder of the first round of the presidential election.[^cite_note-111] The first federal police investigation into the attack concluded that the attacker acted alone, but the investigation "leaves out many issues".[^cite_note-112] Bolsonaro said he did not observe "any effort by \[the] former minister [Sergio Moro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sergio_Moro) to resolve the matter".[^cite_note-\:7-110] Joaquim de Carvalho has said that the police left out of their investigation the search for information by a mastermind of the attack or even a self-attack. For this, expert medical examination of Bolsonaro and review of the medical records would be needed.[^cite_note-113]

### Personal motto

Bolsonaro's personal [motto](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Motto), which he established in 2016, is [John](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gospel_of_John) 8\:32: "[The truth will set you free](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_truth_will_set_you_free)".[^cite_note-114][^cite_note-115][^cite_note-116]

### Protests

Main article: [Ele Não movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ele_N%C3%A3o_movement)

The same weekend he left the hospital, thousands of people took to the streets in dozens of cities in Brazil to protest against Bolsonaro and his political stances, chanting "*_Ele não_*" ("Not him").[^cite_note-globalnews-117] There were also rallies in support of the candidate in sixteen states.[^cite_note-118]
