Bolsonaro was sworn in as president on 1 January 2019, succeeding [Michel Temer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michel_Temer).[^cite_note-119] Bolsonaro began to [get his cabinet together](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bolsonaro_administration_cabinet_members) before his inauguration, choosing the economist [Paulo Guedes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paulo_Guedes) as his economy minister and the astronaut [Marcos Pontes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marcos_Pontes) as his science and technology minister.[^cite_note-120] Bolsonaro said his cabinet would have 15 members; in December when he announced [Ricardo Salles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ricardo_Salles) as a minister, the total was 22. (His predecessor, Michel Temer, had a 29-member cabinet.)[^cite_note-P360Cabinet-121]

Bolsonaro's initial [cabinet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cabinet_of_Brazil) was composed of 16 ministers, two cabinet-level positions, and four presidential secretaries, including Chief of Staff [Onyx Lorenzoni](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Onyx_Lorenzoni).[^cite_note-P360Cabinet-121] Bolsonaro's ministers included [Operation Car Wash](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operation_Car_Wash) judge [Sergio Moro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sergio_Moro) as Justice Minister and congresswoman [Tereza Cristina](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tereza_Cristina) as Minister of Agriculture.[^cite_note-122][^cite_note-123] Bolsonaro placed many army officers in key positions in his cabinet. Before his inauguration, he said he would fill positions in his government based only on technical qualifications and skills rather than ideological sympathy; however, many appointees clashed ideologically with the government during his presidency and fell out of favor with Bolsonaro.[^cite_note-124] By June 2020 the ministers of Justice and [Education](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ricardo_V%C3%A9lez_Rodr%C3%ADguez), the [Secretary of Government](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carlos_Alberto_dos_Santos_Cruz), the head of the [postal service](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Correios) and other government officials had already resigned.[^cite_note-125]

Early in his administration, Bolsonaro focused primarily on domestic and economic issues, ranging from tax reform to changes in social security, but he faced an uphill battle with Congress.[^cite_note-126] Bolsonaro stripped the indigenous affairs agency [FUNAI](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Funda%C3%A7%C3%A3o_Nacional_do_%C3%8Dndio) of the responsibility to identify and demarcate [indigenous lands](31287705.md), arguing that those territories have tiny, isolated populations who would be controlled by NPOs, and proposed to integrate them into the larger Brazilian society. Critics feared that such integration would force [Brazilian Amerindians](55510.md) to suffer [cultural assimilation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cultural_assimilation).[^cite_note-Indigenous_Global_Affairs-5] Argentine President [Mauricio Macri](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mauricio_Macri) was the first foreign leader Bolsonaro received on a state visit to [Brasília](4752.md) after he became president.[^cite_note-127]

The [second inauguration of Nicolás Maduro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Second_inauguration_of_Nicol%C3%A1s_Maduro) in Venezuela took place nine days after [Bolsonaro's inauguration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brazilian_presidential_inauguration#Inauguration_of_Jair_Bolsonaro). The disputed results of the [2018 Venezuelan presidential election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018_Venezuelan_presidential_election) led to the [Venezuelan presidential crisis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Venezuelan_presidential_crisis), as the [National Assembly](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Assembly_\(Venezuela\)) rejected the results, considered Maduro an illegitimate ruler since his first term of office ended, and appointed [Juan Guaidó](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Juan_Guaid%C3%B3) as acting president. Bolsonaro did not attend Maduro's inauguration and recognized Guaidó as the legitimate ruler of Venezuela, alongside [Mauricio Macri](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mauricio_Macri) from Argentina and [Donald Trump](4848272.md) from the US, among others.[^cite_note-128] He said that "We will continue doing everything possible to re-establish order, democracy and freedom there".[^cite_note-129]



Bolsonaro and Trump at the G20 meeting in 2019

After his first year in power, [Bolsonaro's popularity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Opinion_polling_on_the_Jair_Bolsonaro_presidency) steadily declined. A *Datafolha* survey, published on 21 May 2019, showed that 28.6% of respondents described Bolsonaro's administration as "excellent" or "good", 31.3% as "average", and 36.2% as "bad" or "terrible"; 3.9% did not respond. This was the first poll in which more Brazilians disapproved of Bolsonaro's government than approved of it.[^cite_note-130] Meanwhile, after allegations of campaign-finance fraud, Bolsonaro fired [Gustavo Bebianno](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gustavo_Bebianno), a top adviser and general secretary for the president. The party that they both belonged to was accused of diverting public campaign funds to candidates who did not run for office.[^cite_note-131] In November 2019, Bolsonaro left the Social Liberal Party due to conflicts with its leadership. He attempted to form his own party, [Alliance for Brazil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alliance_for_Brazil) ([Portuguese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Portuguese_language): *Aliança pelo Brasil*),[^cite_note-Uol-132] but it failed to gather enough signatures to register at the [Superior Electoral Court](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Superior_Electoral_Court) for the [2020 municipal elections](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2020_Brazilian_municipal_elections) or the [2022 general election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_Brazilian_general_election), leaving Bolsonaro without a party until 2021.[^cite_note-133][^cite_note-134]

Throughout the [COVID-19 pandemic in Brazil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/COVID-19_pandemic_in_Brazil), Bolsonaro and his administration were accused of downplaying the crisis while the number of Brazilians infected by the virus climbed exponentially by mid-2020.[^cite_note-135] Bolsonaro claimed that COVID-19 was no deadlier than "[the flu](19572217.md)" and that his priority was the nation's economic recovery rather than the health crisis.[^cite_note-136] In fact, as at early 2021, the Brazilian economy was bouncing back, albeit somewhat slowly and inconsistently, as the pandemic was still threatening to undo any economic recovery.[^cite_note-137] Bolsonaro continually accused political opponents and the press of exaggerating the threat of the virus and called it a "fantasy" created by the media.[^cite_note-138][^cite_note-139]

In August 2020, in the middle of the pandemic, Bolsonaro's approval rating showed signs of recovery, reaching its highest level since his inauguration.[^cite_note-140] In November 2020 he said he would not take a COVID vaccine if it became available, but he later said he would support any possible vaccine if the [Brazilian Health Agency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brazilian_Health_Regulatory_Agency) deemed it safe.[^cite_note-apnews.com-141] In the same broadcast, he called face masks "the last taboo to fall".[^cite_note-apnews.com-141] In 2020 the [Organized Crime and Corruption Reporting Project](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Organized_Crime_and_Corruption_Reporting_Project) (OCCRP), an international non-governmental organization that investigates crime and corruption, gave Bolsonaro its Person of the Year Award, which "recognizes the individual who has done the most in the world to advance organized criminal activity and corruption". Bolsonaro received the award for "surrounding himself with corrupt figures, using propaganda to promote his populist agenda, undermining the justice system, and waging a destructive war against the Amazon region that has enriched some of the country's worst land owners".[^cite_note-142]



Jair Bolsonaro and [Paulo Guedes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paulo_Guedes) in Brasília, 2020

In early 2021 Bolsonaro's approval ratings fell again, mostly due to the government's response to the COVID-19 pandemic, vaccination controversies, and the concurrent economic crisis that evolved under his watch.[^cite_note-\:6-143] Days after Brazil surpassed Russia as the country worst hit by COVID, Bolsonaro held a political rally in Brasília; his supporters and his own security guards wore masks, but he did not.[^cite_note-144] In June 2021, nationwide protests erupted against Bolsonaro's response to the pandemic; in São Paulo alone there were estimated to be 100,000 protesters on the streets.[^cite_note-145] In July, YouTube removed videos posted by Bolsonaro for spreading false information about the virus. YouTube has reportedly removed 15 videos altogether; one that was removed had shown Brazil's former health minister, [Eduardo Pazuello](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eduardo_Pazuello), comparing the virus to HIV.[^cite_note-146] In other videos, Bolsonaro criticized efforts to stop the spread of the virus, such as wearing masks or taking the vaccine.[^cite_note-147]

By the end of June 2021, more members of the opposition started to call for his impeachment over his handling of the pandemic and spreading [misinformation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/COVID-19_misinformation). The opposition signed a document with multiple accusations, such as blaming Bolsonaro for the deaths of 500,000 Brazilians from COVID-19, stating that his government had blatantly turned down expert advice on tackling the virus, and at least 20 other grievances.[^cite_note-148][^cite_note-149] In July 2021, Bolsonaro claimed on Brazilian radio that his government's greatest achievement was "two and a half years without corruption".[^cite_note-150] In the same month, a scandal dubbed "vaccine-gate" emerged. After months of denying offers of vaccines and bartering the costs, Bolsonaro's government made a deal to buy the unapproved [Covaxin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Covaxin) vaccine from the Indian company Bharat Biotech at a very high price.[^cite_note-151] It was found that the government paid ten times the amount agreed by Bharat Biotech for the vaccine,[^cite_note-152] and that the irregularities were found not in the price of the vaccine, but in a payment of \$45 million to a company in Singapore.[^cite_note-153] In response, the Supreme Court authorized a criminal investigation of Bolsonaro.[^cite_note-154]

In March 2021 Bolsonaro dismissed [Fernando Azevedo e Silva](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fernando_Azevedo_e_Silva) as defense minister and replaced him with [Walter Souza Braga Netto](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Walter_Souza_Braga_Netto); like Bolsonaro, Netto lionized the [1964–1985 military dictatorship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Military_dictatorship_in_Brazil). The day after Netto was appointed, the leaders of the army, air force, and navy all resigned.[^cite_note-155] In April, Bolsonaro claimed that the Brazilian armed forces would "go into the streets" if he ordered them to.[^cite_note-156] In mid-August, the military conducted a ten-minute tank parade in Brasília, with Bolsonaro in attendance. The parade had been held annually in the last 30 years, but tanks had never been sent to the capital before. The parade was announced only a day in advance, and passed by the national congressional building, where lawmakers were due to vote on Bolsonaro's proposed election-related changes hours later. The lawmakers ultimately rejected the changes.[^cite_note-157][^cite_note-158]

On 28 July 2021 Bolsonaro appointed [Ciro Noguiera](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ciro_Nogueira_Lima_Filho), a senator who was implicated in the [Odebrecht corruption case](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Odebrecht_Case), as his [chief of staff](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chief_of_Staff_of_the_Presidency).[^cite_note-159] In early August 2021, Bolsonaro threatened to respond with unconstitutional measures to an investigation over his baseless allegations of fraud vulnerabilities in Brazil's electronic voting system, because he deemed that investigation unconstitutional. Supreme Court Justice [Alexandre de Moraes](53506858.md) had approved the investigation.[^cite_note-160] In mid-August 2021 Bolsonaro warned of a potential "institutional rupture", while urging the Senate to charge de Moraes and another Supreme Court Judge, [Luis Roberto Barroso](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luis_Roberto_Barroso), the leader of the electoral court.[^cite_note-161] On 26 October 2021 a Senate committee approved a report calling for Bolsonaro to face criminal charges, including crimes against humanity, for his handling of the COVID-19 pandemic.[^cite_note-162]



Bolsonaro and Orbán in Budapest, Hungary, in 2022

Without a political party, Bolsonaro began to negotiate his entrance into one in preparation for the [2022 general election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_Brazilian_general_election) (as the [1988 Brazilian Constitution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1988_Brazilian_Constitution) does not allow [independent politicians](2172378.md)).[^cite_note-163] He negotiated with the [Progressistas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Progressistas) (PP), of which he was a member from 1995 to 2003 and from 2005 to 2016,[^cite_note-\:8-164] and the [Social Christian Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Social_Christian_Party_\(Brazil\)) (PSC), of which he was a member from 2016 to 2018,[^cite_note-\:9-165] the [Brazilian Labour Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brazilian_Labour_Party_\(current\)) (PTB),[^cite_note-\:02-166] [Brazilian Woman's Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brazilian_Woman's_Party) (PMB),[^cite_note-167] [Christian Democracy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christian_Democracy_\(Brazil\)) (DC), [Party of National Mobilization](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Party_of_National_Mobilization) (PMN),[^cite_note-\:9-165] the [Republicans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Republicans_\(Brazil\)) and [Patriot](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Patriot_\(Brazil\)) (PATRI).[^cite_note-168] On 30 November 2021 Bolsonaro and his son Senator [Flávio Bolsonaro](58640941.md) joined the [Liberal Party](9373363.md) (PL).[^cite_note-\:8-164] According to political analysts, the choice represented the consolidation of the alliance of Bolsonaro with the *_[Centrão](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Centr%C3%A3o)_*,[^cite_note-169] a large bloc of parties without consistent ideological orientation that supports different sides of the political spectrum to gain political privileges—PL being one of them. Bolsonaro previously made deals with the *Centrão* for support in Congress.[^cite_note-170] On 23 June 2022 Bolsonaro defended his former education minister, [Milton Ribeiro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Milton_Ribeiro), after the latter was arrested on corruption charges.[^cite_note-171]

### 2022 presidential election

Main article: [2022 Brazilian general election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_Brazilian_general_election)

In the second round of the presidential election on 30 October, Bolsonaro was defeated by former president [Luiz Inácio Lula da Silva](99328.md), who took 50.9% of the votes cast.[^cite_note-Buschschlüter_2022-172] Lula had won the most votes in the first round of the election on 2 October, receiving 48.43% of the votes cast; Bolsonaro received 43.20%.[^cite_note-173][^cite_note-174] In a press conference at the [Palácio da Alvorada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pal%C3%A1cio_da_Alvorada) on 1 November, Bolsonaro did not acknowledge his defeat but stated that he would "comply with the Constitution". Regarding the protests by his supporters, he referred to them as "the fruit of indignation and a sense of injustice of how the electoral process unfolded", while calling on them to remain peaceful and not block roads. Shortly after his speech, the Supreme Court stated that by authorizing the transition of power he had recognized the results,[^cite_note-175][^cite_note-176] paving the way for the transition two days after Lula was recognized as the winner.[^cite_note-177][^cite_note-178] Bolsonaro left for the United States on 30 December to avoid taking part in the swearing-in ceremony of Lula, leaving Vice President Hamilton Mourão as the acting president.[^cite_note-179]

The documentary *[Apocalypse in the Tropics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Apocalypse_in_the_Tropics)* (2024) shows how evangelical pastor [Silas Malafaia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Silas_Malafaia) had a defining influence on Bolsonaro, while mustering support from Brazil's evangelical movement, in the path to Bolsonaro losing the election and in the protests and attempted insurrection that followed.[^cite_note-apocalypse-180]

## Post-presidency (2023–present)



Bolsonaro in 2023 visiting the state of [Paraná](229411.md)

On 30 December 2022, one day before the end of his term, Bolsonaro arrived in the [US state](18618239.md) of [Florida](18933066.md) and resided in [Kissimmee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kissimmee) for several months.[^cite_note-181][^cite_note-182] On 8 January 2023 [his supporters attacked](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/8_January_Bras%C3%ADlia_attacks) the buildings of the [Supreme Court of Brazil](3359076.md), the [National Congress](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Congress_of_Brazil) and the [Planalto Presidential Palace](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pal%C3%A1cio_do_Planalto) in an attempt to instigate a military coup d'état and reinstate Bolsonaro as president.[^cite_note-auto1-183] While the riots were going on, President Lula blamed Bolsonaro in a press conference.[^cite_note-auto1-183][^cite_note-auto-184] Bolsonaro condemned the protesters in a Twitter post on 9 January,[^cite_note-btweet-185] and denied responsibility.[^cite_note-auto-184] In February 2023 Bolsonaro announced that he would be returning to Brazil in March.[^cite_note-186] This would be the first time Bolsonaro had returned to the country since December 2022.[^cite_note-187] Bolsonaro had entered the United States on a diplomatic visa which expired on 31 January, but the family applied for tourist visas to extend their stay in Florida.[^cite_note-188]

Bolsonaro returned to Brazil in March 2023 for the first time since his supporters stormed the Supreme Court, Congress, and the presidential palace two months before. Bolsonaro has stated that he returned to the country to help his party and asserted that he intended to campaign for the [2024 elections](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Brazilian_municipal_elections).[^cite_note-189] On 14 April 2023, Supreme Court judge [Alexandre de Moraes](53506858.md) ordered Bolsonaro to submit himself for questioning with the [Federal Police](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brazilian_Federal_Police) over the storming of the Congress.[^cite_note-190]

In July 2024, the Federal Police charged Jair Bolsonaro with money laundering and criminal conspiracy related to undeclared diamonds that he allegedly received from [Saudi Arabia](349303.md) while he was in office.[^cite_note-\:11-191]

### Ineligibility, charges of coup planning, conviction and return to federal custody

Main articles: [Political ineligibility of Jair Bolsonaro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Political_ineligibility_of_Jair_Bolsonaro), [2022 Brazilian coup plot](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_Brazilian_coup_plot), [Operation Counter-coup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operation_Counter-coup), [AP 2668](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AP_2668), and [2025 Brazil–United States diplomatic dispute](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Brazil%E2%80%93United_States_diplomatic_dispute)



Bolsonaro showing his [electronic ankle monitor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Electronic_ankle_monitor) at the [National Congress](690287.md); after the incident, [Alexandre de Moraes](53506858.md) requested an explanation from him, as he was prohibited from giving interviews—otherwise, he would be immediately arrested, but Moraes ultimately deemed it just an "isolated irregularity".

On 30 June 2023, the [Superior Electoral Court](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Superior_Electoral_Court) barred Bolsonaro from running for public office until 2030 as a result of his attempts to undermine the validity of Brazil's 2022 democratic election, as well as for abuse of power with regard to using government channels to promote his campaign.[^cite_note-DW30623-192][^cite_note-\:10-193] The decision applies to municipal, state and federal elections for the next eight years. He was prosecuted on several fraud charges in the 2022 elections and events linked to the 8 January attack on federal government buildings. The decision came after a 5–2 vote in favour of conviction. Following the decision, Bolsonaro stated his intentions to appeal.[^cite_note-\:10-193][^cite_note-ElPais30623-194]

On 31 October 2023, Bolsonaro was again convicted by the Superior Electoral Court over [abuse of power](4308572.md) for using [Brazil's Independence Day](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Independence_Day_\(Brazil\)) ceremony to promote himself as a candidate which is banned under the Brazilian law. Former defence minister [Walter Braga Netto](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Walter_Braga_Netto), who attended the ceremony, and also ran for vice president on Bolsonaro's ticket, was convicted by the Court and ruled disqualified to run for any office for the next eight years.[^cite_note-195]

In February 2024, the Federal Police raided former government officials and ordered Bolsonaro to hand in his passport over accusations that he and his allies tried to overturn the results of the 2022 election and planned a [coup d'état](25914154.md).[^cite_note-196] In March 2024, witness documents released by the Superior Electoral Court were made public. According to two military officials, Bolsonaro had plotted to overturn the 2022 results and presented top military officials a plan to carry out a coup with the goal of keeping him in power.

In his testimony the former [Brazilian Army](1047559.md) commander [Marco Antônio Freire Gomes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marco_Ant%C3%B4nio_Freire_Gomes) said that he had warned Bolsonaro the army would not tolerate "any act of institutional rupture", and added that Bolsonaro's actions could result in his arrest. The former [Brazilian Air Force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brazilian_Air_Force) commander [Carlos de Almeida Baptista Júnior](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carlos_de_Almeida_Baptista_J%C3%BAnior) testified that he tried to dissuade Bolsonaro of "any extreme measure" and expressed his belief that Freire Gomes was instrumental in avoiding the use of a legal document that Bolsonaro presented in several meetings in December 2022 to overturn the results of the election. Baptista Júnior further said that the then [Brazilian Navy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brazilian_Navy) commander [Almir Garnier Santos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Almir_Garnier_Santos) told Bolsonaro he would put his troops at his disposal, and commented: "If the commander \[Freire Gomes] had agreed, possibly, a coup d'état attempt would have taken place."[^cite_note-197][^cite_note-198][^cite_note-199]

In late March 2024 *[The New York Times](30680.md)* released footage from internal security cameras in the Hungarian embassy in Brasília showing Bolsonaro and his aides and bodyguards entering the building on 12 February and leaving the place on 14 February. This happened after his Brazilian and Italian passports were confiscated, and Bolsonaro was prohibited from leaving the country due to an investigation about an alleged plot to carry out a military coup. Bolsonaro was welcomed by Hungarian ambassador to Brazil Miklós Halmai who reportedly sent the embassy's local employees a message to work from home during those dates.[^cite_note-200]

Following the report, the Supreme Court gave Bolsonaro a 48-hour deadline to explain his stay in the embassy,[^cite_note-201] and the Federal Police announced it was opening an investigation into the incident; which could have been viewed as an attempt to escape justice via [diplomatic asylum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Diplomatic_law#Diplomatic_asylum), since embassies are typically considered inviolable and host countries cannot enforce their law inside them without permission.[^cite_note-202] Media commentators and people on social media speculated this hypothesis was likely to be true citing Bolsonaro and Hungarian prime minister [Viktor Orbán](750582.md) are both right-wing populist politicians who share a personal friendship, and Hungary's previous granting of asylum to the sanctioned former Macedonian prime minister [Nikola Gruevski](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nikola_Gruevski).[^cite_note-203][^cite_note-204] Additionally, the [Ministry of Foreign Affairs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Foreign_Affairs_\(Brazil\)) summoned Halmai to give explanations about Bolsonaro's two-night stay in the embassy.[^cite_note-205]

On 19 March 2024 the Federal Police formally accused Bolsonaro of fraud on his COVID-19 vaccine records.[^cite_note-206] In November 2024 he and 36 others were formally charged with plotting a coup d'état, violent abolition of the democratic rule of law, and criminal organisation.[^cite_note-207][^cite_note-208] In February 2025 Bolsonaro and 33 others were formally charged with plotting to assassinate Lula da Silva and Supreme Court Justice [Alexandre de Moraes](53506858.md).[^cite_note-209] On 26 March the Supreme Federal Court ordered Bolsonaro and seven of his associates to stand trial over the charges.[^cite_note-210]

On 21 November 2024 the Federal Police indicted Bolsonaro and 36 other people related to Bolsonaro's defeat in the 2022 presidential elections. He is alleged to have known of a plot to assassinate his successor, Luiz Inácio Lula da Silva, and to overturn the election result.[^cite_note-211]

The trial against Bolsonaro and 80 other officials began in Brasília on 19 May 2025.[^cite_note-212]

On 9 July 2025 US president [Donald Trump](4848272.md) criticized the criminal prosecution of Bolsonaro, accused Brazil of being an unfair trading partner, and claimed the [50% tariff imposition](78448911.md) was still less than what the US needed for a "level playing field"—despite the US trade surplus with Brazil.[^cite_note-213] This led to comparisons about [federal prosecution of Donald Trump](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Federal_prosecution_of_Donald_Trump_\(election_obstruction_case\)) involving the [scandal with Stormy Daniels](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stormy_Daniels%E2%80%93Donald_Trump_scandal)[^cite_note-214] and [Lula's arrest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arrest_of_Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva),[^cite_note-215] and Trump's [eligibility](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Presidential_eligibility_of_Donald_Trump) after the 6 January [United States Capitol attack](66307490.md)[^cite_note-216] versus Bolsonaro's [ineligibility](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ineligibility_of_Jair_Bolsonaro) after the [8 January Brasília attacks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/8_January_Bras%C3%ADlia_attacks).[^cite_note-217] Bolsonaro could request [US political asylum](7195971.md)—especially given that Trump labelled Brazil's charges a "[witch hunt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Witch_hunt)"—but would need safe‑conduct to leave Brazil and must prove credible political persecution. US authorities would then decide whether to grant asylum;[^cite_note-218] diplomatic tensions and travel restrictions would make the outcome uncertain.[^cite_note-219]

On 18 July 2025 police again searched his home and ordered him to wear an [electronic ankle tag](87233.md).[^cite_note-220] On 26 August he was placed under 24-hour surveillance after being designated as a "flight-risk".[^cite_note-221]

Wikinews logo

Wikinews has related news:&#x20;

- [ Brazil's supreme court sentences former president Jair Bolsonaro to 27 years in prison](https://en.wikinews.org/wiki/Brazil's%20supreme%20court%20sentences%20former%20president%20Jair%20Bolsonaro%20to%2027%20years%20in%20prison)

On 11 September 2025, the Supreme Federal Court sentenced Bolsonaro to 27 years and 3 months in prison over the coup plot.[^cite_note-222][^cite_note-223]

On 22 November 2025, Bolsonaro was arrested, transferred to the headquarters of the [Federal Police of Brazil](2361410.md) in Brasilia and ordered by Supreme Court Justice Alexandre de Moraes to be transferred back to prison after he attempted to remove the electronic ankle tag and escape house arrest.[^cite_note-federalcustody-30][^cite_note-nov22arrest-31][^cite_note-prisonorder-32] Bolsonaro blamed his actions on a nervous breakdown and hallucinations caused by a change in his medication.[^cite_note-224] On November 24 the Supreme Federal Court unanimously voted to keep Bolsonaro in police custody.[^cite_note-policecustodyremain-225][^cite_note-remainsinpolicecustody-226] On November 25 the Supreme Court rejected his final appeals, declared the case to have reached *[res judicata](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Res_judicata)* and ordered the beginning of the enforcement of the prison sentence.[^cite_note-\:12-33] On December 19, he was allowed temporary release to undergo a [hernia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hernia) operation.[^cite_note-227]

On 1 January 2026, [Supreme Federal Court](3359076.md) rejected Bolsonaro's request to convert his [prison sentence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prison_sentence) to [house arrest](247643.md). Bolsonaro's lawyers submitted the request on 31 December 2025 quoting "real risk of a sudden worsening" regarding his health as the reason why the sentence should be converted. Judge de Moraes said "there has been no worsening" of Bolsonaro's health.[^cite_note-228] On 15 January 2026, Justice Alexandre de Moraes ordered the transfer of Bolsonaro to the Federal District's 19th Military Police Battalion at the Papuda Penitentiary Complex. Moraes determined that Bolsonaro be held in a “state quarters” room, with better conditions suited to his health condition.[^cite_note-229]

On 13 March 2026, Bolsonaro was hospitalized and admitted to the [ICU](6332859.md) after feeling unwell during the night. He experienced dizziness, high fever, a drop in oxygen saturation, and chills. He was diagnosed with [bacterial bronchopneumonia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bronchopneumonia). On 24 March 2026, Justice Alexandre de Moraes granted Bolsonaro temporary humanitarian house arrest. The house arrest will last for 90 days from his discharge from the hospital. Moraes also ordered that Bolsonaro must wear an electronic ankle monitor, may not receive visitors except for family members and lawyers, and may not have any contact with mobile phones or social media.[^cite_note-\:13-34] On 3 July 2026, Moraes ordered the extension of Bolsonaro's humanitarian house arrest.[^cite_note-cnnbrasil.com.br-35]

## Political positions

Main article: [Bolsonarism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bolsonarism)



Bolsonaro supported the [European Union–Mercosur Free Trade Agreement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European_Union%E2%80%93Mercosur_Free_Trade_Agreement), which would form one of the world's largest free trade areas.

Bolsonaro's political views have been described as [Brazilian nationalist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brazilian_nationalist) and [right-wing populist](8354382.md) in nature, and he himself as an advocate of [far-right politics](221220.md).[^cite_note-230][^cite_note-opendemocracy-231] His supporters say that his views are more aligned with traditional right-wing conservatism.[^cite_note-232] His electorate is mainly formed by adults above the age of 34, the working middle to upper class (mainly in the southeast region of the country), conservatives in general, college graduates, some centrists and the [Christian right](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christian_right).[^cite_note-233] According to some polls, Bolsonaro's main support comes from the [southeast](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southeast_Region,_Brazil), [central-west](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Central-West_Region,_Brazil), and [south](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Region,_Brazil) regions of Brazil. His voters are usually male and white, with a noticeable gender gap, with Bolsonaro polling poorly among female voters (mustering only 18% support with this demographic).[^cite_note-globalnews-117][^cite_note-234][^cite_note-235] Just before the 2018 election, however, it was reported that female support for him had risen to 27%.[^cite_note-236]

Bolsonaro is viewed as an [anti-abortion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anti-abortion),[^cite_note-237] [anti-establishment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anti-establishment), and pro-gun politician, voicing opposition to most forms of [gun laws in Brazil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gun_laws_in_Brazil), arguing that law-abiding citizens have the right to self-defence, especially those living in rural areas.[^cite_note-238] According to *The Washington Post*, "Homicides hit a [record high](15348345.md) of 63,880 last year ... Bolsonaro's solution is zero tolerance. He has called for police to use more lethal force and wants to relax gun laws so that average citizens can defend themselves."[^cite_note-environment-6] Bolsonaro often rejects accusations made against him of [misogyny](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Misogyny) and [homophobia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Homophobia), and says he is not far-right but simply right-wing.[^cite_note-estadaomarco-239]

Bolsonaro is known for his strong opposition to [left-wing politics](18499.md). Most notably, he has been a vocal opponent of [same-sex marriage](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Same-sex_marriage_in_Brazil),[^cite_note-noticias.terra.com.br-12] environmental regulations,[^cite_note-Atkins_2018-240] abortion,[^cite_note-osul.com.br-14] [affirmative action](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Affirmative_action) (particularly racial quotas),[^cite_note-Política-15] as well as immigration,[^cite_note-241] particularly from Haiti, Africa and the Middle East, which he once called "the scum of humanity".[^cite_note-opendemocracy-231] He has also opposed [drug liberalization](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Drug_liberalization),[^cite_note-epoca2-16] land reforms,[^cite_note-242] and secularism at the federal level,[^cite_note-epoca2-16] among other things.[^cite_note-243] Additionally, he made statements in defence of the [Brazilian military regime](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brazilian_military_regime),[^cite_note-244] (US-backed dictatorship known for human-rights violations).[^cite_note-245] He argues that torture is a "legitimate practice" and says that he would try to pass new legislation regarding the introduction of life imprisonment to the Brazilian penal code.[^cite_note-246] Bolsonaro supports the [privatization](24661.md) of state-owned companies and advocates [free-market](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Free-market) policies,[^cite_note-\:1-247] although critics have stated that his policy-making record does not in fact show him to be a supporter of [economic liberalism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Economic_liberalism).[^cite_note-\:2-248]



Bolsonaro and [Charles](125248.md), then Prince of Wales, in October 2019

In a 2017 interview with the journalist Claudio Dantas Sequeira from *[O Antagonista](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/O_Antagonista)*, Bolsonaro said that his views are directly aligned with the centrist to right-wing United States citizens' views on gun ownership, abortion, gender politics, and trade, despite the "left-leaning media frenzy" against him. He reiterated that he intends to reverse some disarmament laws, improve public security, and also improve trade ties with the United States, which he said were broken during [Luiz Inácio Lula da Silva](99328.md)'s and [Dilma Rousseff](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dilma_Rousseff)'s administrations.[^cite_note-249]

During his long political career, Bolsonaro expressed views regarded as being far-right.[^cite_note-250] He has made statements that some people considered insulting, homophobic,[^cite_note-dw-homophobia-251] violence-inciting,[^cite_note-globo-violence-inciting-252][^cite_note-usnews-253][^cite_note-washingtonpost-254] misogynistic, sexist,[^cite_note-gardian-sexist-255][^cite_note-usnews-253][^cite_note-washingtonpost-254] racist,[^cite_note-Gavin_Fernando-256][^cite_note-usnews-253][^cite_note-washingtonpost-254] or anti-refugee.[^cite_note-anti-refugee-257] Other controversial political stances expressed by Bolsonaro have been the defence of the death penalty, which is banned under the [Constitution of Brazil](26847507.md) of 1988, and of radical interventionism in Brazil by the military, along with an imposition of a Brazilian military government.[^cite_note-epoca2-16][^cite_note-258]

The journalist [Glenn Greenwald](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Glenn_Greenwald) called Bolsonaro "the most misogynistic, hateful elected official in the democratic world".[^cite_note-259] [News.com.au](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/News.com.au) wondered whether Bolsonaro was "the world's most repulsive politician".[^cite_note-Gavin_Fernando-256] The news magazine *[The Economist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Economist)* referred to him as a "radical", "religious nationalist", a "right-wing demagogue", and "apologist of dictators".[^cite_note-260] [Federico Finchelstein](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Federico_Finchelstein), a scholar on fascism and populism, has considered Bolsonaro, as he would link violence to [austerity](684037.md) and [neoliberal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neoliberal) economic ideas, to be the most similar leader to [Augusto Pinochet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Augusto_Pinochet) to come out from the young South American democracies.[^cite_note-261] Leonardo Fontes, a postdoctoral researcher at the Brazilian Centre for Analysis and Planning, characterized Bolsonaro's economic views as [authoritarian neoliberalism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Authoritarian_neoliberalism).[^cite_note-262]

As President of Brazil, Bolsonaro professed to be an open admirer of then-President of the United States, [Donald Trump](4848272.md), during [his first term](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/First_Trump_administration). During Bolsonaro's campaign some observers saw similarities between the two's ideals, hardline attacks and a reputation for incendiary rhetoric, as well as social media presence. Because of this, Bolsonaro has been called the Brazilian equivalent of Trump or the "Trump of the Tropics".[^cite_note-Foreign_Staff-263]

### Honorific order given to Olavo de Carvalho

On 1 May 2019 Bolsonaro awarded [Olavo de Carvalho](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Olavo_de_Carvalho), a conservative writer and far-right [conspiracy theorist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Conspiracy_theorist),[^cite_note-264][^cite_note-265] the [Order of Rio Branco](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Order_of_Rio_Branco) honorific order. Carvalho, who wrote books about [leftist](18499.md) politics in Brazil and modern issues in general, was openly admired by Bolsonaro and sarcastically called by journalists his [guru](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Guru).[^cite_note-266][^cite_note-267][^cite_note-268][^cite_note-269] Carvalho was sometimes harshly critical of Bolsonaro, even calling him "dumb".[^cite_note-270][^cite_note-271][^cite_note-272][^cite_note-273]

### Views on the Brazilian military dictatorship



Bolsonaro and his son [Eduardo](58054973.md) hold up a Brazilian flag during a ceremony to mark the 50th anniversary of the [1964 military coup d'état](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1964_Brazilian_coup_d'%C3%A9tat), 1 April 2014.

Throughout his political career, Bolsonaro has made several admiring comments about the [Brazilian military dictatorship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brazilian_military_regime), which ruled the country from 1964 to 1985. He said in 1993, eight years after the return of democracy, that the military regime had "led to a more sustainable and prosperous Brazil".[^cite_note-nyt-8] He has publicly referred to the military dictatorship as a "glorious" period in Brazil's history,[^cite_note-R7.com-274] and that under the military dictatorship, Brazil enjoyed "20 years of [order and progress](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Order_and_Progress)".[^cite_note-R7.com-274] In December 2008 he said that "the error of the dictatorship was that it tortured, but did not kill".[^cite_note-275]

Bolsonaro has also repeatedly made admiring comments about a number of other Latin American dictatorships. He praised Peruvian president [Alberto Fujimori](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alberto_Fujimori) as a role model for his use of military intervention via [self-coup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1992_Peruvian_constitutional_crisis) against the judiciary and legislature.[^cite_note-nyt-8] In a 1998 interview with *Veja* magazine, Bolsonaro praised the [Chilean dictatorship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Military_dictatorship_of_Chile_\(1973%E2%80%9390\)) of [Augusto Pinochet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Augusto_Pinochet), and said the Pinochet regime, which killed over 3,000 Chilean citizens, "should have killed more people".[^cite_note-276] In 1999 Bolsonaro said that [Hugo Chávez](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hugo_Ch%C3%A1vez) represented "hope for Latin America", comments that became a matter of controversy during the 2018 campaign, when Bolsonaro presented himself as a harsh critic of *[Chavismo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chavismo)*.[^cite_note-277] In 2019, already in power, Bolsonaro commended Paraguayan dictator [Alfredo Stroessner](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alfredo_Stroessner) as a "visionary" and "statesman", drawing immediate criticism, particularly due to multiple allegations of [pedophilia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pedophilia) against Stroessner.[^cite_note-278][^cite_note-279] The comments were made in front of Paraguayan president [Mario Abdo Benítez](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mario_Abdo_Ben%C3%ADtez), himself a child of Stroessner's personal secretary, Mario Abdo Benítez Sr.[^cite_note-280]

Speaking before his vote in favour of [President Dilma Rousseff's impeachment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Impeachment_process_against_Dilma_Rousseff) amid the [massive corruption scandal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operation_Car_Wash), Bolsonaro paid homage to Colonel [Brilhante Ustra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carlos_Alberto_Brilhante_Ustra), an agent of [Brazil's military dictatorship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brazilian_military_government), and announced on the floor of the Chamber of Deputies that he was dedicating his pro-impeachment vote to Ustra's memory. Ustra had headed the [DOI-CODI](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/DOI-CODI) torture unit where Rousseff was allegedly tortured during the military dictatorship. The left-wing deputy [Jean Wyllys](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jean_Wyllys) spat at him after his statement during the same session. The congressman claimed to have suffered homophobic offenses from Jair Bolsonaro and his allies.[^cite_note-281][^cite_note-282]

In a TV interview with *Câmera Aberta* in the 1990s, Bolsonaro said that if he ever became president, he would use this as an opportunity to shut down the National Congress and instigate a military coup himself. As of 2018, he appeared to have changed his mind, and said that if someone becomes the head of the country, it would be through voting.[^cite_note-283] In March 2019 Bolsonaro stated that the [1964 coup d'état](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1964_Brazilian_coup_d'%C3%A9tat), which overthrew President [João Goulart](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jo%C3%A3o_Goulart),[^cite_note-1964coup-284] was not a [coup](25914154.md), and that 31 March, the day the coup was installed, should be "properly commemorated".[^cite_note-285][^cite_note-286][^cite_note-287]

### Foreign policy

See also: [List of international presidential trips made by Jair Bolsonaro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_international_presidential_trips_made_by_Jair_Bolsonaro)



Bolsonaro with [Boris Johnson](19065069.md), then the British prime minister, 20 September 2021

During the 2018 presidential campaign, Bolsonaro said he would make considerable changes to Brazil's foreign relations, saying that the "*[Itamaraty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Foreign_Affairs_\(Brazil\))* needs to be in service of the values that were always associated with the Brazilian people". He also said that the country should stop "praising dictators" and attacking democracies, such as the United States, Israel and Italy.[^cite_note-foreignpolicy-288] In early 2018 he affirmed that his "trip to the five democratic countries the United States, Israel, Japan, [South Korea](27019.md), and [Taiwan](25734.md) showed who we will be and we would like to join good people". Bolsonaro showed distrust towards China throughout the presidential campaign claiming they "\[want to] buy Brazil",[^cite_note-289][^cite_note-290] although Brazil recorded a US\$20 billion trade surplus with China in 2018, and China is only the 13th largest source of foreign direct investment into Brazil.[^cite_note-ft-291] Bolsonaro said he wished to continue to do business with the Chinese but he also said that Brazil should "make better \[economic] deals" with other countries, with no "ideological agenda" behind it.[^cite_note-estadaomarco-239] His stance towards China has also been interpreted by some as an attempt to curry favor with the [Trump](4848272.md) administration to garner concessions from the US.[^cite_note-ft-291] However, Bolsonaro mostly changed his position on China after he took office, saying that the two countries were "born to walk together" during his visit to Beijing in October 2019.[^cite_note-\:5-292][^cite_note-293] He also said that Brazil would stay out of the ongoing [China-US trade war](57055272.md).[^cite_note-\:5-292] According to [Oliver Stuenkel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oliver_Stuenkel), Bolsonaro's stance on global politics evolved from anti-China to anti-Western during his presidency.[^cite_note-294] In 2022 he praised the role of BRICS during the [2008 financial crisis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2008_financial_crisis) and described BRICS cooperation as a "factor of stability and prosperity in the international situation".[^cite_note-brics2008-295]

Bolsonaro said that his first international trip as president would be to [Israel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brazil%E2%80%93Israel_relations).[^cite_note-palestine-296] Bolsonaro also said that the [State of Palestine](241405.md) "is not a country, so there should be no embassy here", adding that "you don't negotiate with terrorists."[^cite_note-palestine-296] The announcement was warmly received by the prime minister of Israel, [Benjamin Netanyahu](56472.md), who welcomed Bolsonaro to Israel in March 2019 during the final weeks of a re-election campaign,[^cite_note-297] but met condemnation from the [Arab League](52625.md), which warned Bolsonaro it could damage diplomatic ties.[^cite_note-298] "I love Israel", Bolsonaro said in [Hebrew](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hebrew_language) at a welcoming ceremony, with Netanyahu at his side, at [Ben Gurion Airport](113149.md) in [Tel Aviv](31453.md).[^cite_note-299]



Bolsonaro with Chinese President [Xi Jinping](2017814.md) in October 2019

Bolsonaro also praised US President [Donald Trump](4848272.md) and [his foreign policy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Foreign_policy_of_the_first_Donald_Trump_administration),[^cite_note-foreignpolicy-288] and has been called "the tropical Trump".[^cite_note-300] His son [Eduardo](58054973.md) has indicated that Brazil should distance itself from [Iran](14653.md), sever ties with [Nicolás Maduro](7161476.md)'s government in [Venezuela](32374.md) and relocate [Brazil's embassy in Israel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_diplomatic_missions_in_Israel) to [Jerusalem](16043.md), although he never committed to his plan to move the embassy and instead opened a [trade office](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trade_office) in the city.[^cite_note-301][^cite_note-302] Bolsonaro is widely considered the most [pro-American](24296062.md) candidate in Brazil since the 1980s. PSL members said that if elected, he would dramatically improve [relations between the United States and Brazil](11633777.md).[^cite_note-\:3-18] During an October 2017 campaign rally in Miami, he saluted the [American flag](11447.md) and led chants of "USA! USA!" to a large crowd.[^cite_note-303] US National Security Advisor [John Bolton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_R._Bolton) praised Bolsonaro as a "like-minded" partner and said his victory was a "positive sign" for Latin America.[^cite_note-304] Bolsonaro had a fractious relationship with US President [Joe Biden](145422.md),[^cite_note-305] and subsequently deepened ties with [Russia](25391.md), emphasizing his neutrality over the [2022 Russian invasion of Ukraine](79400268.md).[^cite_note-russia-20]

At the regional level, Bolsonaro praised Argentine President [Mauricio Macri](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mauricio_Macri) for ending the 12-year rule of [Néstor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/N%C3%A9stor_Kirchner) and [Cristina Fernández de Kirchner](1644780.md), which he saw as similar to Lula and Rousseff. Although he does not have plans to leave the [Mercosur](25869317.md), he criticized it for prioritizing ideological issues over economic ones.[^cite_note-306] A staunch anti-communist, Bolsonaro has condemned [Cuba](5042481.md)'s former leader [Fidel Castro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fidel_Castro) and the regime on that island as of 2018.[^cite_note-churchill-307][^cite_note-308]

Bolsonaro praised British Prime Minister [Winston Churchill](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Winston_Churchill), saying that he had learnt from Churchill: "Patriotism, love for your fatherland, respect for your flag – something that has been lost over the last few years here in Brazil ... and governing through example, especially at that difficult moment of the Second World War."[^cite_note-churchill-307] Bolsonaro said he was open to the possibility of hosting a [US military base](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_military_deployments) in Brazil to counter Russian influence in the region.[^cite_note-309] With the intention to persuade Trump to make Brazil a [NATO](21133.md) member in March 2019, Bolsonaro said: "the discussions with the United States will begin in the coming months".[^cite_note-310][^cite_note-311][^cite_note-312][^cite_note-313]



Bolsonaro with Russian President [Vladimir Putin](32817.md) in November 2019

With formal US support for Brazil's entry to [OECD](33853117.md) in May 2019, Bolsonaro said, "currently, all 36 members of the organization support the entry of the country, fruit of confidence in the new Brazil being built, more free, open and fair".[^cite_note-314][^cite_note-315][^cite_note-316] In October 2019, on a state visit to China, he announced the end of the need for visas for Chinese and Indian entry into Brazil. Brazil had already removed the need for visas for people from the US, Canada, Japan, and Australia.[^cite_note-317]

### Environment and climate change



The deforestation rate in Brazil surged by 72% during Bolsonaro's time in office, reflecting that Amazon development was his key policy position.

[^cite_note-Axios_20221031-318]

[^cite_note-TerraBrasilis_1988-319]

Brazil has the world's largest tropical rainforest in the [Amazon basin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amazon_basin). According to *[The Washington Post](102226.md)*, "Bolsonaro is a powerful supporter of [agribusiness](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Agriculture_in_Brazil) ... and is likely to favor profits over preservation. ... Bolsonaro has chafed at foreign pressure to safeguard [the Amazon rainforest](48139.md), and he served notice to international nonprofit groups such as the [World Wide Fund for Nature](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Wide_Fund_for_Nature) that he will not tolerate their agendas in Brazil. He has also come out strongly against [lands](31287705.md) reserved for [indigenous tribes](55510.md). Bolsonaro advisers additionally say that he plans to expand [nuclear](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nuclear_activities_in_Brazil) and hydroelectric power into the Amazon."[^cite_note-environment-6]

Bolsonaro rejects the [scientific consensus on climate change](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Scientific_consensus_on_climate_change). He repeatedly threatened to withdraw from the [Paris Agreement](30242372.md) during his campaign.[^cite_note-320][^cite_note-\:0-321] Even before taking office, he backed out of Brazil's offer to host the [2019 UN Climate Change Conference](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_Climate_Change_conference#2019:_COP_25/CMP_15/CMA_2,_Madrid,_Spain).[^cite_note-322] [Ernesto Araújo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ernesto_Ara%C3%BAjo), the new Minister of Foreign Affairs appointed by Bolsonaro, has called global warming [a plot](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Global_warming_conspiracy_theory) by "cultural Marxists",[^cite_note-323] and eliminated the Climate Change Division of the ministry.[^cite_note-\:0-321] Two departments of the [Ministry of the Environment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_the_Environment_\(Brazil\)) dealing with [climate change in Brazil](66412282.md) and mitigation and one dealing with deforestation were also eliminated.[^cite_note-\:0-321]

In April 2019 the [American Museum of Natural History](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Museum_of_Natural_History) canceled an event honouring Bolsonaro after facing heavy public criticism, including from New York Mayor [Bill de Blasio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bill_de_Blasio). The museum's directorate justified its decision in a statement, "With mutual respect for the work and goals of our organizations, we jointly agreed that the Museum is not the optimal location for the Brazilian-Am. Chamber of Commerce gala dinner. This traditional event will go forward at another location on the original date and time."[^cite_note-324] Bolsonaro supported plans to open the Reserva Nacional do Cobre e Associados (Renca) [Amazonian](48139.md) reserve in Brazil's northern states of [Pará](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Par%C3%A1) and [Amapá](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amap%C3%A1) to commercial mining.[^cite_note-325]



Bolsonaro, French President [Emmanuel Macron](43671127.md) and Saudi Crown Prince [Mohammad bin Salman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mohammad_bin_Salman) at the 2019 G20 Osaka summit

[Destruction of the Amazon rainforest](19732090.md) increased by 88% for the month of June 2019, during Bolsonaro's first year as president, as compared with the previous year, according to the [National Institute for Space Research](30875637.md) (INPE). Bolsonaro rejected the agency's data as false. The INPE director was fired after he rebutted Bolsonaro's criticism of the institute.[^cite_note-326][^cite_note-327][^cite_note-328][^cite_note-329] The Bolsonaro administration decreased government efforts to combat illegal logging, ranching and mining in the Amazon. Government enforcement actions such as fines, warnings and the confiscation or destruction of illegal equipment in protected areas decreased by 20% in the first half of 2018 compared to the first half of 2017.[^cite_note-330]

His damage to the Amazon has widely been described by indigenous groups, human rights groups, politicians, academics and journalists as an [ecocide](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ecocide) and a [genocide](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Genocide_of_Indigenous_peoples_in_Brazil#Affected_tribes).[^cite_note-331][^cite_note-332][^cite_note-333][^cite_note-334] Indigenous chiefs and human rights organizations have submitted an Article 15 communication to the [International Criminal Court](14880.md) for crimes against humanity and genocide for harm to Indigenous people and destruction of the Amazon.[^cite_note-335][^cite_note-336][^cite_note-337] Another has been submitted for ecocide by indigenous chiefs.[^cite_note-338][^cite_note-339]

### Christianity and secularism

Bolsonaro is a member of the [Catholic Church](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Catholic_Church_in_Brazil) (while his wife and one of his sons are [Evangelical Christians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Evangelical_Christian))[^cite_note-340] and is registered as one with the [Superior Electoral Court](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Superior_Electoral_Court). In 2016, he was [re-baptized](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rebaptism) along with three of his sons, [Flávio](58640941.md), [Carlos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carlos_Bolsonaro) and [Eduardo](58054973.md), in the [Jordan River](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jordan_River) by a member of a [Pentecostal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pentecostalism) church. The sociologist Christina Vital of the [Fluminense Federal University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fluminense_Federal_University) stated that this act was more than an expression of conversion to [Evangelicalism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Evangelicalism) and was meant to create an ambiguous religious identity, through which the Bolsonaro family could appeal to the various groups of voters.[^cite_note-341]

Bolsonaro reportedly attended a [Baptist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baptist) church for 10 years.[^cite_note-342] In a 2017 speech, Bolsonaro stated, "God above everything. There is no such thing as a [secular state](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Secular_state). The state is Christian, and any minority that is against this has to change, if they can."[^cite_note-343] He later evolved his position to keeping the country a secular state during the first round of the Brazilian presidential elections: "We are going to make a government for everyone, regardless of religion. Even for atheists. We have almost 5% atheists in Brazil, and they have the same needs that others have."[^cite_note-noticias.uol.com.br-344]

### Views on women

In an interview with *Zero Hora* in 2015, Bolsonaro argued that men and women should not receive the same salaries, because women get pregnant, adding that he believes federal law mandating paid [maternity leave](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maternity_leave) harms work productivity.[^cite_note-345] Bolsonaro has denied saying that women should receive less than men; he claims it was statistical data by [IBGE](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/IBGE).[^cite_note-346] In a public speech in April 2017, Bolsonaro said he had five children, that the first four were male and that for the fifth he produced a daughter out of "a moment of weakness".[^cite_note-347]



Bolsonaro arguing with federal deputy [Maria do Rosário](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maria_do_Ros%C3%A1rio) in the [Chamber of Deputies](1589485.md), 14 September 2016

Bolsonaro provoked controversy for a series of remarks made to and about Federal Deputy and former Human Rights Minister [Maria do Rosário](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maria_do_Ros%C3%A1rio). During a Congressional debate, Bolsonaro said that minors should be [treated as adults](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trial_as_an_adult) if they commit heinous crimes such as murder or rape, to which Maria do Rosário responded by calling him a "rapist".[^cite_note-348][^cite_note-349][^cite_note-350] Bolsonaro then stated that Congresswoman Rosário was "not worth raping; she is very ugly".[^cite_note-gardian-sexist-255] The remarks drew considerable condemnation throughout Brazil. In the aftermath of these remarks, Bolsonaro was tried and convicted in a Federal court in September 2015 on counts of [hedonic damages](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hedonic_damages) against Rosário.[^cite_note-351] In June 2016 the Federal Supreme Court responded to a complaint filed by the [Attorney General](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Attorney_General_of_Brazil) and decided to open two criminal actions against Bolsonaro. The Supreme Court ruled that he had potentially incited rape and defamed the honour of his fellow Deputy. He faced a penalty of up to six months of jail and a fine.[^cite_note-352] Ultimately in August 2017, an appellate court upheld a lower court's verdict which found Bolsonaro guilty and sentenced him to pay a fine to Rosário of R\$10,000 (roughly equivalent to US\$2,500).[^cite_note-353] This lawsuit was dismissed by the Supreme Federal Court as Bolsonaro was inaugurated as president in 2019 and acquired [immunity from prosecution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Legal_immunity).[^cite_note-354]

### Views on homosexuality



[Gay Pride](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gay_Pride) parade in São Paulo, June 2011

In May 2002, after then-President Fernando Henrique Cardoso attended a pro-gay-marriage gathering, Bolsonaro said, "If I see two men kissing in the street, I will beat them."[^cite_note-355] He later publicly defended beating gay children, saying, "If your child starts to become like that, a little bit gay, you take a whip and you change their behaviour." He also said, "If a kid hangs out with someone who uses marijuana, he'll end up snorting, and if he hangs out with gay person, he definitely is going to turn into a faggot."[^cite_note-356]

In a June 2011 interview with *[Playboy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Playboy)*, Bolsonaro said, "I would be incapable of loving a gay son", and added that he would prefer any gay son of his "to die in an accident".[^cite_note-357] In an interview that same month with *Jornal de Notícias*, Bolsonaro [linked homosexuality](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LGBT_grooming_conspiracy_theory) to [paedophilia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pedophilia), saying, "many of the children who are adopted by gay couples will be abused by these couples".[^cite_note-358] He further argued that Brazil does not need legislation specifically targeting homophobia, because "most homosexuals are murdered by their respective pimps at hours when good citizens are already asleep".[^cite_note-epoca2-16] In the British actor [Stephen Fry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stephen_Fry)'s 2013 documentary *Out There*, Bolsonaro said, "no father is ever proud of having a gay son" and "we Brazilians do not like homosexuals".[^cite_note-359]



Bolsonaro and his son Eduardo, together with fellow-congressman [Marco Feliciano](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marco_Feliciano), watching as a couple of protesters for LGBT rights kiss on the floor of the Committee on Culture of the Chamber of Deputies, May 2016

In a March 2016 video interview for *[Vice](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vice_Media)* with [Elliot Page](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elliot_Page), an openly lesbian actress at the time, Page asked Bolsonaro whether he should have been beaten as a child (alluding to Bolsonaro's public statements that gay children should be beaten). Bolsonaro replied, "You are very nice. If I were a cadet in the military academy and saw you on the street, I would whistle at you. All right? You are very pretty."[^cite_note-advocate.com-360] Bolsonaro added, "Over time, due to liberal habits, drugs, with women also working, the number of homosexuals has really increased."[^cite_note-advocate.com-360]

On 9 November 2017 the Court of Justice for the State of Rio de Janeiro sentenced Bolsonaro to pay a fine of R\$150,000 for [hate speech](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hate_speech) because of televised comments he made in 2011 to the *[Custe o Que Custar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Custe_o_Que_Custar)* television programme, when Bolsonaro stated that "there is no risk" of his family producing a homosexual child because his children had a "good education". Judge Luciana Teixeira said that the deputy had abused his right of free expression to commit a wrongful act. "You cannot deliberately attack and humiliate, ignoring the principles of equality, just because you invoke freedom of expression", said the judge.[^cite_note-361]

However, on 11 January 2016, when he began to present himself as a pre-candidate to the [Presidency of Brazil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Presidency_of_Brazil), Bolsonaro began to moderate his discourse on gay people by publishing a video on his official YouTube channel:

> I have nothing to do with anyone's behaviour. If the husband and wife later decide to live with their partner, to form a couple, to live with a person of the same sex, they should and be happy. But we cannot state that, because of our omission in the Parliament, children become homosexuals in the future, or have such homosexual behaviour in the future, because the influence of school. That is unacceptable.[^cite_note-362]

Since then, Bolsonaro states he has nothing against gays and that he only fights against the "gay kit" in schools.[^cite_note-363] On 4 October 2018, for example, Bolsonaro said:

> Each person, after a certain age, is responsible for their acts, and will take care of their life. For six-year-old children, this does not hold true. A father does not want to come home and see his son playing with a doll because of the influence of school. Homosexuals will be happy if I become president.[^cite_note-364]

That same month, shortly before the first round of the Brazilian presidential elections, he said: "We are going to make a government for everybody. For gays, and some gays are fathers, who are mothers. It is a work for everyone".[^cite_note-noticias.uol.com.br-344] After being elected president, when asked by [William Bonner](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/William_Bonner_\(newscaster\)) in the *[Jornal Nacional](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jornal_Nacional)* about what he would say to those who are more prejudiced and aggressive against gays, Bolsonaro replied: "The aggression against a fellow man has to be punished in the way of law. And if \[such aggression is committed] for a reason like this, you have to have your sentence increased."[^cite_note-365]

### Views on political violence



Bolsonaro visits the [Federal Senate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brazilian_Senate), March 2016.

On multiple occasions, Bolsonaro has publicly endorsed physical violence as a legitimate and necessary form of political action. In 1999, when he was 44 years old and a representative in the Brazilian Congress, Bolsonaro said during a TV interview that the only way of "changing" Brazil was by "[killing thirty thousand people](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dirty_War), beginning with [Fernando Henrique Cardoso](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fernando_Henrique_Cardoso)" (then President of Brazil).[^cite_note-366] During the 2018 campaign, he stated during a rally in [Acre](229497.md) that the local "petralhas" (a derogatory term for members of the Workers' Party) would be "shot"; according to his aides, the statement was a "jok".[^cite_note-367] One week before the second round, Bolsonaro said during a speech that in his administration "petralhas" and "reds" (i.e. leftists) would be arrested, purged or taken to the "corner of the beach", a term that was later revealed to mean a Navy base where dissidents of the [Brazilian military dictatorship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brazilian_military_dictatorship) were murdered.[^cite_note-uol.com.br-80]

In 1999, talking about [Chico Lopes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chico_Lopes?action=edit\&redlink=1), a former president of the Brazilian Central Bank who invoked his right to remain silent during a Congress hearing, Bolsonaro declared himself in favor of torture in this sort of situation.[^cite_note-368] Asked about this phrase years later, Bolsonaro said: "Ask the father of a kidnapped child what he would like him to do to discover \[where the kid is]. You have to take brutal measures, which some consider torture".[^cite_note-369]

### Views on race

Throughout his political career, Bolsonaro has made numerous statements that have been described as racist.[^cite_note-370] In 2011, when asked by the Afro-Brazilian singer [Preta Gil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Preta_Gil) on TV show *Custe o Que Custar* (CQC) what he would do if one of his sons had a black girlfriend, he answered that he "would not discuss promiscuity" and that there was "no such risk", because his children were "very well educated". Bolsonaro later claimed that he was misunderstood and denied promoting racism, although he stood by his attacks on gay people.[^cite_note-371]

In 2017, in an event at the Hebraica club in Rio de Janeiro, Bolsonaro promised to abolish all [indigenous](55510.md) and [Quilombola](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Quilombola) territories in Brazil, saying that he would not cede "a centimiter" of land to these groups. He also claimed to have visited a [quilombo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Quilombo), a settlement formed by descendants of enslaved people, accusing [Afro-Brazilians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Afro-Brazilians) who lived there of being lazy and unproductive. The politician also claimed that the "least heavy" of the quilombolas weighed seven [arrobas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arroba), a unit of measure that is used in Brazil to weigh cattle.[^cite_note-372] These comments have been criticized as an attempt to [dehumanize](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dehumanization) black Brazilians.[^cite_note-373]

Bolsonaro is a strong opponent of [affirmative action](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Affirmative_action), and has criticized the Brazilian racial quotas system in universities as a way of dividing society that is at odds with equality between citizens.[^cite_note-374] In 2011, he was accused of racism after questioning the capabilities of Black and indigenous graduates who benefitted from affirmative action, claiming that he would not fly on an airplane piloted by one of them, nor accept a doctor who was admitted to a university through racial quotas to perform surgery on him.[^cite_note-Política-15]

### Birth control for the poor

Bolsonaro provoked considerable controversy for public remarks made in July 2008, where he proposed to provide poor people with birth control methods, who he suggested might be too uneducated to understand family planning education. Bolsonaro said:

> I wish Brazil had a family planning programme. It's not even worthy to talk about education when most of these \[poor] people are not prepared to receive education, therefore they won't educate themselves. Only rigid birth control can save us from chaos. An educated man and woman will hardly desire an extra child with the sole purpose of engaging in a social welfare assistance programme \[as it is nowadays]. We need to adopt a rigid birth control policy. We can't make demagogic speeches any longer, proposing bills and means of government to support these poor people \[who] are increasingly proliferating throughout the country. ... People who aren't prepared to have children, shouldn't have them. This is what I stand for and I'm not worried about getting votes in the future. It's past time to discuss a policy to contain this demographic explosion, otherwise, we'll keep voting in this [Chamber](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chamber_of_Deputies) only matters such as [Bolsa Família](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bolsa_Fam%C3%ADlia), loans for the poor, gas vouchers, etc. Methods \[of birth control] have to be provided for those who, unfortunately, are ignorant and have no means to control their offspring. Because we \[as upper-middle class] can control ours. Poor people don't control \[theirs].[^cite_note-375][^cite_note-376][^cite_note-377][^cite_note-378]

As a Congressman, Bolsonaro put forward three bills trying to remove "virtually all" legal restrictions to surgical sterilization via the public health system, including the reduction of "the minimum age of sterilization to 21 years".[^cite_note-379] None of the bills were voted through.

### Elections

Bolsonaro has endorsed conspiracy theories of [voter fraud](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Voter_fraud) in past elections, including claims that attempts were made to rig the 2018 presidential election against him;[^cite_note-380] he has also questioned the outcome of the [2020 United States presidential election](48410011.md).[^cite_note-waldron-381] During his presidency, he has repeatedly challenged the legitimacy of [electronic voting](371301.md) and advocated the use of paper ballots in the 2022 election.[^cite_note-nugent-382] Bolsonaro said that he would not accept the results of the 2022 election if electoral reforms were not implemented.[^cite_note-waldron-381][^cite_note-phillips-383] Most experts on Brazilian politics, including defence minister [Celso Amorim](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Celso_Amorim), questioned the likelihood of a coup attempt, and polls found that few Bolsonaro supporters would likely endorse a coup.[^cite_note-nugent-382][^cite_note-waldron-381] In May 2022, [Central Intelligence Agency](5183633.md) director [William Burns](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/William_J._Burns_\(diplomat\)) warned Bolsonaro against any further attacks on Brazil's electoral system.[^cite_note-phillips-383] The 2022 election occurred days after the success of far-right politician [Giorgia Meloni](29228976.md) in the [2022 Italian general election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_Italian_general_election),[^cite_note-384] with analysts noting that Bolsonaro performed better than expected during the first round of elections and that his party's success had the potential to moderate Lula's government if Lula won the election.[^cite_note-385][^cite_note-386]

## &#x20;Personal life



Bolsonaro with his two sons, Eduardo and Flávio, in the late 1980s

Bolsonaro has been married three times and has five children. His first wife was Rogéria Nantes Braga (with whom he has three sons: [Flávio](58640941.md), [Carlos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carlos_Bolsonaro), and [Eduardo](58054973.md)). His second marriage was with Ana Cristina Valle (with whom he has a son, Renan). In 2007 he married his third wife, [Michelle de Paula Firmo Reinaldo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michelle_Bolsonaro), with whom he has a daughter named Laura.[^cite_note-387][^cite_note-388]

While working in Congress, Bolsonaro hired Michelle as a secretary and over the next two years she received promotions and her salary more than tripled. He was forced to fire her after the [Supreme Federal Court](3359076.md) ruled that [nepotism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nepotism) is illegal in the public administration.[^cite_note-389][^cite_note-390] In 2018 Bolsonaro and his wife lived in [Barra da Tijuca](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Barra_da_Tijuca), Rio de Janeiro.[^cite_note-391]

Bolsonaro has three granddaughters, two by Flávio[^cite_note-392] and one by Eduardo.[^cite_note-393] Bolsonaro is [Catholic](606848.md).[^cite_note-394] On 25 October 2018 he was presented with an honorary black belt by [Robson Gracie](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Robson_Gracie) despite never having trained [Brazilian jiu-jitsu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brazilian_jiu-jitsu).[^cite_note-395]

On January 9, 2023, Bolsonaro was taken to [AdventHealth Celebration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AdventHealth_Celebration) with abdominal pain.[^cite_note-396]

On September 16, 2025, Bolsonaro's son said he was transported to a hospital in [Brasília](4752.md) after reportedly feeling sick.[^cite_note-397] The next day, it was announced that Bolsonaro was diagnosed with an early type of [skin cancer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Skin_cancer).[^cite_note-398]
